   118	Non-goals and later Phase 6 boundaries
   119	--------------------------------------
   120	
   121	Phase 6A adds no SQL, migration, table, repository, service, formatter, CLI,
   122	preview command, operator-review persistence, approval, rejection, activation,
   123	execution, scheduler work, queue, worker, campaign, recommendation, experiment,
   124	or Phase 4D materialization.  It does not claim profitability, statistical
   125	significance, repeatability, campaign success, or follow-up authorization.
   126	
   127	Persistence, explicit operator review, approval/rejection, activation, and
   128	execution remain distinct possible Phase 6B--6F concerns.  This contract does
   129	not authorize, prescribe, or partially implement any of them.
     1	Phase 6C Recommendation Campaign Follow-Up Proposal Administrative Review
     2	===========================================================================
     3	
     4	Purpose
     5	-------
     6	
     7	Phase 6C records one explicit immutable operator decision about one exact
     8	persisted Phase 6B follow-up proposal.  The decision is administrative review
     9	evidence only.  ``approved`` means that an operator approved the persisted
    10	advisory proposal for possible consideration by a later, separately authorized
    11	phase.  ``rejected`` is equally immutable and auditable.
    12	
    13	Neither outcome activates or executes follow-up work.  Phase 6C has no
    14	activation adapter, queue, schedule, scheduler signal, worker launch,
    15	experiment creation/modification, recommendation creation, campaign-success
    16	claim, profitability claim, or statistical-validation authority.
    17	
    18	Immutable contract and identity
    19	-------------------------------
    20	
    21	``RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalReview`` is a pure, database-free,
    22	non-assignable value.  Contract version 1 binds:
     1	Phase 6D Recommendation Campaign Follow-Up Proposal Administrative Approval
     2	============================================================================
     3	
     4	Purpose and recovered roadmap
     5	-----------------------------
     6	
     7	Phase 6D records one immutable administrative approval for one exact eligible
     8	Phase 6C follow-up-proposal review event.  The committed Phase 6A roadmap
     9	separates Phase 6B--6F into persistence, review, approval/rejection,
    10	activation, and execution concerns.  Phase 6C uses ``approved`` and
    11	``rejected`` as its review outcomes, but explicitly defines an approved review
    12	as evidence for possible consideration by a later separately authorized
    13	phase.  Phase 6D is that distinct administrative approval boundary; it is not
    14	the later activation or execution boundary.
    15	
    16	Only an exact persisted Phase 6C ``approved`` review is eligible.  A rejected,
    17	missing, malformed, stale, identity-inconsistent, or unsupported review cannot
    18	be approved.  Phase 6D never rebuilds the Phase 6A proposal or recomputes the
    19	Phase 5 assessment/policy decision.  It loads the authoritative Phase 6C
    20	record, whose validated repository reload in turn verifies the exact persisted
    21	Phase 6B proposal and complete Phase 6A identity.
    22	
    23	Immutable domain contract
    24	-------------------------
    25	
    26	``RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApproval`` is a pure, database-free,
    27	non-assignable contract version 1 value.  It binds:
     8	Superseded by: None
     9	
    10	## 1. Context and problem statement
    11	
    12	Phase 6C records an immutable approved or rejected administrative review for
    13	one exact persisted Phase 6B proposal. Its approved outcome is deliberately
    14	non-authorizing evidence for possible later consideration. The Phase 6A
    15	roadmap also keeps persistence, review, approval/rejection, activation, and
    16	execution as distinct possible Phase 6B–6F concerns. The next increment needs
    17	one explicit approval authority without turning reviewed evidence into
    18	activation, execution, scheduling, or experiment capability.
    19	
    20	## 2. Decision
    21	
    22	Phase 6D defines a pure immutable administrative approval value, additive
    23	migration 044, an exact-binding append-only repository, and one transactional
    24	service.
    25	
    26	- Only an exact persisted Phase 6C `approved` review is eligible. Rejected,
    27	  missing, malformed, stale, or identity-inconsistent reviews fail closed.
    28	- Contract version 1 binds the review-event ID; exact review and proposal
    29	  version/canonical/hash identities; approved review and approval decisions;
    30	  approver; reason; and fixed negative operational semantics.
    31	- Canonical text is authoritative, byte-length framed, locale-independent,
    32	  and bounded. The tagged hash is only an accelerator.
    33	- Migration 044 stores one immutable approval per review/proposal, retains
    34	  restrictive foreign keys, and verifies the full Phase 6C/6B chain in an
    35	  invoker-rights insert trigger with pinned safe `search_path`.
    36	- A review-event-scoped transaction advisory lock serializes approval attempts.
    37	  First persistence is `recorded`, exact replay is `existing_identical`, and
    38	  any changed payload for that review conflicts.
    39	- The service loads and validates the exact Phase 6C record, checks an explicit
    40	  expected review hash and eligibility, builds the pure approval, and persists
    41	  in one transaction.
    42	- Runtime access is SELECT, payload-column INSERT, and sequence USAGE only.
    43	  Existing approvals cannot be updated, deleted, expired, or superseded.
    44	- No CLI, activation adapter, execution adapter, queue, scheduler call,
    45	  worker launch, or experiment/model/recommendation mutation is added.
    46	
    47	Administrative approval is evidence only. It is explicitly not follow-up or
    48	execution authorization.
    49	
    50	## 3. Rationale and decision drivers
    51	
    52	- Preserve the roadmap's separate review and approval authorities despite
    53	  Phase 6C's approved/rejected review terminology.
    54	- Consume authoritative persisted upstream records without recomputing Phase
    55	  5 policy or Phase 6A proposal truth.
    24	enum class RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalDecision
    25	{
    26	    approved
    27	};
    28	
    29	struct RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalIdentity
    30	{
    31	    const int contractVersion;
    32	    const std::string canonicalText;
    33	    const std::string hash;
    34	
    35	    RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalIdentity(
    36	        const RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalIdentity&) =
    37	        default;
    38	    RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalIdentity(
    39	        RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalIdentity&&) = default;
    40	    bool operator==(
    41	        const RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalIdentity&) const =
    42	        default;
    43	
    44	private:
    45	    RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalIdentity(
    46	        int contractVersion,
    47	        std::string canonicalText,
    48	        std::string hash);
    49	    friend struct RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalBuilder;
    50	};
    51	
    52	// Phase 6D is immutable administrative approval evidence. Approval remains
    53	// deliberately distinct from every later operational authority.
    54	struct RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApproval
    55	{
    56	    static constexpr bool readOnly = true;
    57	    static constexpr bool databaseFree = true;
    58	    static constexpr bool persistent = false;
    59	    static constexpr bool administrativeApproval = true;
    60	    static constexpr bool eligibleReviewRequired = true;
    61	    static constexpr bool activated = false;
    62	    static constexpr bool executionAuthorized = false;
    63	    static constexpr bool followUpAuthorized = false;
    64	    static constexpr bool queued = false;
    65	    static constexpr bool scheduled = false;
    66	    static constexpr bool schedulerStarted = false;
    67	    static constexpr bool schedulerSignaled = false;
    68	    static constexpr bool workersStarted = false;
    69	    static constexpr bool experimentsCreated = false;
    70	    static constexpr bool experimentsModified = false;
    71	    static constexpr bool campaignSuccessDeclared = false;
    72	
    73	    const RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalIdentity identity;
    74	    const long long reviewEventId;
    75	    const int reviewContractVersion;
    76	    const std::string reviewCanonicalText;
    77	    const std::string reviewIdentityHash;
    78	    const long long followUpProposalId;
    79	    const int proposalContractVersion;
    80	    const std::string proposalCanonicalText;
    81	    const std::string proposalIdentityHash;
    82	    const RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalReviewDecision reviewDecision;
    83	    const RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalDecision decision;
    84	    const std::string approverIdentity;
    85	    const std::string reasonText;
    86	
    87	    RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApproval(
    88	        const RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApproval&) = default;
    89	    RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApproval(
    90	        RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApproval&&) = default;
   125	    const std::string& reasonText)
   126	{
   127	    std::ostringstream output;
   128	    output.imbue(std::locale::classic());
   129	    output
   130	        << "experiment_recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_approval_v1"
   131	        << ";approval_contract_version="
   132	        << kRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalContractVersion
   133	        << ";read_only=true"
   134	           ";database_free=true"
   135	           ";persistent=false"
   136	           ";administrative_approval=true"
   137	           ";eligible_review_required=true"
   138	           ";activated=false"
   139	           ";execution_authorized=false"
   140	           ";follow_up_authorized=false"
   141	           ";queued=false"
   142	           ";scheduled=false"
   143	           ";scheduler_started=false"
   144	           ";scheduler_signaled=false"
   145	           ";workers_started=false"
   146	           ";experiments_created=false"
   147	           ";experiments_modified=false"
   148	           ";campaign_success_declared=false"
   149	        << ";review_event_id=" << reviewEventId
   150	        << ";review_contract_version=" << reviewContractVersion
   151	        << ";review_canonical=" << LengthPrefixed(reviewCanonicalText)
   152	        << ";review_identity_hash=" << LengthPrefixed(reviewIdentityHash)
   153	        << ";review_decision="
   154	        << RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalReviewDecisionText(
   155	               reviewDecision)
   156	        << ";follow_up_proposal_id=" << followUpProposalId
   157	        << ";proposal_contract_version=" << proposalContractVersion
   158	        << ";proposal_canonical=" << LengthPrefixed(proposalCanonicalText)
   159	        << ";proposal_identity_hash=" << LengthPrefixed(proposalIdentityHash)
   160	        << ";decision="
   161	        << RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalDecisionText(decision)
   162	        << ";approver=" << LengthPrefixed(approverIdentity)
   163	        << ";reason=" << LengthPrefixed(reasonText);
   164	    return output.str();
   165	}
    31	RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalRequest
    32	ValidateRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalRequest(
    33	    const RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalRequest& request)
    34	{
    35	    if (request.reviewEventId <= 0)
    36	        throw std::invalid_argument(
    37	            "recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_approval_review_event_id_invalid");
    38	    if (!IsValidCanonicalHash(request.expectedReviewIdentityHash))
    39	        throw std::invalid_argument(
    40	            "recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_approval_expected_review_hash_invalid");
    41	    return request;
    42	}
    43	
    44	RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalPersistResult
    45	ApproveRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposal(
    46	    pqxx::connection& connection,
    47	    const RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalRequest& request)
    48	{
    49	    const auto validated =
    50	        ValidateRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalRequest(request);
    51	    pqxx::work transaction{connection};
    52	    if (!RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalReviewSchemaExists(transaction) ||
    53	        !RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalSchemaExists(transaction))
    54	        throw std::runtime_error(
    55	            "recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_approval_schemas_required");
    56	    const auto persistedReview =
    57	        FindRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalReview(
    58	            transaction, validated.reviewEventId);
    59	    if (!persistedReview)
    60	        throw std::runtime_error(
    61	            "recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_approval_review_not_found");
    62	    if (persistedReview->review.identity.hash !=
    63	        validated.expectedReviewIdentityHash)
    64	        throw std::runtime_error(
    65	            "recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_approval_review_identity_mismatch");
    66	    if (persistedReview->review.decision !=
    67	        RecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalReviewDecision::approved)
    68	        throw std::runtime_error(
    69	            "recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_approval_review_not_eligible");
    70	    const auto approval =
    71	        BuildRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApproval(
    72	            persistedReview->reviewEventId, persistedReview->review,
    73	            validated.approverIdentity, validated.reasonText);
    74	    auto result = PersistRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApproval(
    75	        transaction, approval);
    76	    transaction.commit();
    77	    return result;
    78	}

   147	            '%I.enforce_recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_approval_provenance()',

   153	        CROSS JOIN LATERAL aclexplode(coalesce(

   165	        RAISE EXCEPTION 'function NULL-ACL fallback fixture invalid';

   172	                  current_schema())])) THEN
   173	        RAISE EXCEPTION 'follow-up proposal approval trigger execution context unsafe';
   174	    END IF;

   179	    IF NOT has_sequence_privilege('pqxx', approval_sequence, 'USAGE') OR
   180	       has_sequence_privilege('pqxx', approval_sequence, 'SELECT') OR
   181	       has_sequence_privilege('pqxx', approval_sequence, 'UPDATE') THEN
   182	        RAISE EXCEPTION 'follow-up proposal approval sequence privilege incorrect';
   183	    END IF;

codex
The full empty-database runner check exposed a pre-existing repository limitation: migrations begin at `004`, and migration `012` expects a baseline `model` table, so `migrate_lstm_db.sh` cannot bootstrap an empty database by itself (`relation "model" does not exist`). The disposable database was dropped by the exit trap. I’m checking the documented baseline/restore contract to classify this accurately rather than attributing it to Phase 6D.

# LSTM Database Migrations

Run migrations whenever a branch adds or changes LSTM database schema objects.

```bash
./migrate_lstm_db.sh
```

Defaults:

- `LSTM_DB_HOST=127.0.0.1`
- `LSTM_DB_NAME=LSTM`
- `LSTM_DB_ADMIN_USER=$USER`, or `vjp` if `$USER` is unset

Override example:

```bash
LSTM_DB_HOST=127.0.0.1 LSTM_DB_NAME=LSTM LSTM_DB_ADMIN_USER=vjp ./migrate_lstm_db.sh
```

The migration user must have enough PostgreSQL privileges to create tables,
create indexes, and grant privileges to the runtime user `pqxx`.

The LSTM runtime user `pqxx` should not need schema-creation privileges after
migrations are applied. It only needs DML privileges on runtime tables such as
`inference_eval_result`, `experiment`, and `experiment_analysis_result`.

Applied migrations are tracked in `schema_migrations`:

- `version text primary key`
- `filename text not null`
- `checksum text not null`
- `applied_at timestamptz not null default now()`

Experiment scheduling tables are created by:

- `005_experiment_scheduler.sql`: `experiment`
- `006_experiment_analysis.sql`: `experiment_analysis_result`

Recommendation conversion and campaign-approval history is created by:

- `036_experiment_recommendation_conversion_proposal.sql`:
  `experiment_recommendation_conversion_proposal`
- `037_experiment_recommendation_conversion_review.sql`:
  `experiment_recommendation_conversion_review_decision`
- `038_experiment_recommendation_conversion_execution.sql`:
  `experiment_recommendation_conversion_execution`
- `039_experiment_recommendation_conversion_activation.sql`:
  `experiment_recommendation_conversion_activation`
- `040_experiment_recommendation_campaign_approval.sql`:
  `experiment_recommendation_campaign_approval`
- `041_experiment_recommendation_campaign_materialization.sql`:
  `experiment_recommendation_campaign_materialization` and
  `experiment_recommendation_campaign_materialization_member`, with
  invoker-rights provenance and deferred completeness enforcement
- `042_experiment_recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal.sql`:
  immutable exact Phase 6A proposal manifests and ordered members for Phase 6B
  read-only operator preview
- `043_experiment_recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_review.sql`:
  one immutable approved/rejected Phase 6C administrative review event per
  exact persisted Phase 6B proposal, without action authority
- `044_experiment_recommendation_campaign_follow_up_proposal_approval.sql`:
  one immutable Phase 6D administrative approval per exact eligible approved
  Phase 6C review, without operational authorization or action authority

These append-only tables record manually prepared proposals and their explicit
operator review decisions. An approval is administrative evidence for possible
later conversion; it is not an experiment queue, does not create an experiment,
and is not read by the scheduler. The greatest review-decision ID for a proposal
defines its current review disposition. That generated sequence-ID order, not
transaction commit time or the descriptive timestamp, is authoritative.
An explicit Step 4 conversion records the exact approving decision and creates
one paused experiment. It does not queue, start, or schedule that experiment.
An explicit Step 5 activation records a separate immutable audit event and
changes only that existing experiment from `paused/train` to `pending/train`.
It creates no experiment, starts no worker, and adds no scheduler dependency on
Phase 4C tables.
Phase 4C Step 6 adds no schema object or privilege. Its read-only workflow view
joins these existing audit records with current experiment lifecycle state and
reports deterministic integrity diagnostics.
Phase 4D Steps 1 and 2 add no schema object or privilege. Their
read-only campaign planner and review consume one explicit completed ranking
snapshot plus existing
recommendation and Phase 4C workflow evidence in a PostgreSQL read transaction.
They do not persist a plan or advance a sequence.
Phase 4D Step 3 reconstructs that exact plan and review in the same transaction
that inserts one immutable operator approval or rejection. Runtime ``pqxx``
has ``SELECT``, column-limited payload ``INSERT``, and sequence ``USAGE`` only;
it cannot provide generated IDs/timestamps or update, delete, or truncate
approval history. Canonical review text is authoritative, identical retries
return the existing row, and changed payload for the same review conflicts.
Campaign approval does not create or modify an experiment and does not execute
the campaign.
Phase 4D Step 4 atomically reconstructs one approved campaign and creates or
reuses only its exact ordered Phase 4C conversion-proposal set. One immutable
manifest and ordered member links preserve approval, ranking, review, and
proposal provenance. Runtime access is append-only and column-limited.
First-time materialization reconstructs current authoritative evidence; exact
retry validates and returns the immutable manifest because its Phase 4C
proposals intentionally change later planning evidence.
Materialization creates no conversion review, execution, activation, or
experiment and does not involve the scheduler or workers.
Phase 4D Step 5 adds no schema object or privilege. Its read-only campaign
handoff projection treats the Step 4 manifest/member rows as authoritative
membership, selects the greatest Phase 4C review-decision ID for current
disposition, and validates linked proposal, execution, activation, and
experiment evidence in one read transaction. It advances no sequence and
uses `REPEATABLE READ` for one consistent snapshot; it never repairs or
progresses a workflow.
Phase 4D Step 6 also adds no schema object or privilege. One explicit operator
command validates the exact immutable Step 4 member set and atomically appends
one ordinary Phase 4C review row per member. A deterministic request ID binds
the materialization, decision, operator, reason, and operation version for exact
retry recognition. The greatest Phase 4C review-decision ID remains authoritative;
no campaign-level status authority, execution, activation, experiment, scheduler,
or worker behavior is added.
Phase 5 Step 1 adds no schema object or privilege. Its explicit confirmed
campaign command validates one exact Phase 4D materialization and uses the
existing Phase 4C execution transaction primitive to create all corresponding
``paused/train`` experiments and immutable conversion-execution rows atomically.

codex
## Executive verdict

Not ready to commit. The implementation is mechanically strong and its focused verification passes, but Phase 6D does not define a decision meaningfully distinct from Phase 6C’s existing approval. This is a blocking authority-boundary defect.

Starting/final state remained valid:

- Branch: `phase6d`
- HEAD: `9689d7d14f6354610525ca8dc9e430af06350862`
- `phase6c-complete` is an ancestor and is exactly HEAD.
- Index remained empty.
- No unrelated worktree changes appeared.

## Blocking finding

Phase 6D currently duplicates Phase 6C’s approval semantics.

The committed Phase 6A roadmap distinguishes review from approval/rejection as possible concerns ([Phase 6A roadmap](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase6ARecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposal.rst:127>)). But committed Phase 6C already says that an operator “approved the persisted advisory proposal,” recording reviewer, reason, and an immutable `approved`/`rejected` decision ([Phase 6C contract](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/Phase6CRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalReview.rst:7>)).

Phase 6D then records another `approved` decision with another operator and reason, but defines no different question, role, policy, threshold, separation-of-duty requirement, or governance consequence. Its canonical literally contains both `review_decision=approved` and `decision=approved` ([approval canonical](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/Sources/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApproval.cpp:149>)).

The uncommitted ADR acknowledges the collision but resolves it only by asserting that the authorities are separate ([ADR-0009](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/docs/architecture/adr/ADR-0009-phase-6d-follow-up-proposal-administrative-approval.md:12>)). It does not define what the second authority decides.

### Phase 6C versus Phase 6D answers

1. Phase 6D currently records only a second positive attestation after Phase 6C approval; it records no distinct decision.
2. Type names distinguish “review” and “approval,” but fields, canonical identity, service semantics, and tests do not encode different governance questions.
3. Yes, operators and later phases could reasonably confuse them.
4. It adds a second record and signature, but not demonstrably new governance evidence. The same person may perform both stages.
5. Approved-only eligibility is mechanically enforced, but its policy correctness depends on first defining the second authority.
6. A Phase 6C rejection is correctly and permanently ineligible.
7. Phase 6D remains fully non-operational and non-authorizing.
8. The committed roadmap supports separate possible concerns abstractly, but Phase 6C already consumed the approve/reject decision. The new uncommitted ADR cannot bootstrap its own justification.
9. Naming and documentation require correction after the authority is defined. `ApproveRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposal` is particularly ambiguous.
10. This is a blocking architectural defect, not a documentation-only concern.

## Technical assessment

Subject to resolving that blocker:

- Domain contract: mechanically sound. Versioning, immutable/non-assignable values, classic-locale formatting, byte framing, field order, size bounds, hash recomputation, canonical-first equality, UTF-8/control validation, fixed negative authority, and exclusion of database metadata are correct.
- Migration 044: additive, repeatable, restrictive, and provenance-complete. Both foreign keys use restrictive deletion; copied Phase 6C/6B provenance and approved eligibility are trigger-validated.
- Privileges: correct least-privilege shape—`SELECT`, column-level `INSERT`, and sequence `USAGE`; no table-level insert, update, delete, truncate, generated-column insertion, PUBLIC access, or direct trigger-function execution. Sequence ACL fallback correctly uses lowercase `s`.
- Trigger: `SECURITY INVOKER`, pinned `search_path`, correct function identity, and no upstream mutation.
- Repository: validates before write, locks the review-event conflict domain transactionally, reloads exact Phase 6C and transitively Phase 6B, implements deterministic first-write/replay/conflict behavior, and treats canonical content—not hashes—as authoritative.
- Concurrency: equivalent requests converge; conflicting requests yield one winner and one deterministic conflict. Advisory-key collisions only over-serialize unrelated work.
- Service: one transaction loads the exact review, checks the expected identity hash and eligibility, constructs the value, persists it, commits, and returns the hydrated authoritative row.
- CLI sequencing: omitting main-program CLI integration is consistent with the documented non-operational increment. It also means Phase 6D currently has no operator-facing route.
- No activation, execution, scheduling, queueing, worker, experiment/model, continuation, profitability, or campaign-success authority was introduced.

## Corrections made

Only two test files were changed during review:

- [Phase 6D pure tests](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/Tests/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalTests.cpp:100>): added a fixed golden hash, valid UTF-8 coverage, and explicit DEL rejection.
- [Phase 6D migration tests](</Volumes/Developer SSD/ExpertAdvisor/Tests/ExperimentRecommendationCampaignFollowUpProposalApprovalMigrationTests.sql:159>): added forced NULL-ACL fallback checks for functions, tables, and sequences, explicitly using lowercase `s`.

No production/domain behavior was changed.

## Verification results

Passed:

- Phase 6D pure tests with C++20 warnings-as-errors.
- Phase 6D migration/repository/service suite, including clean Phase 6 chain, upgrade from existing 6B/6C rows, repeatability, ACLs, eligibility, replay, conflicts, concurrency, rollback, malformed storage, read-only behavior, and unchanged upstream/scheduler fixtures.
- Explicit independent NULL-ACL fallback verification.
- Phase 6A pure regression.
- Phase 6B repository/preview regression.
- Phase 6C pure and repository/presentation regressions.
- `xcodebuild -list -project ExpertAdvisor.xcodeproj`
- `plutil -lint ExpertAdvisor.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`
- Project membership: each new `.cpp` has one file reference and membership in both targets; each header has one file reference.
- `git diff --check`

The isolated Phase 6 “clean install” passed. A full empty-database run of every repository migration could not bootstrap because the repository begins at migration `004`, while migration `012` expects the historical baseline `model` table. It failed before reaching Phase 6D. The disposable database and leaked runner temporary file were removed. This is inherited migration-baseline behavior, not a migration-044 failure.

## Release build and operational safety

The Release build was not run. Final OS-only inspection found:

- Active scheduler PID `43031` with wrapper PIDs `43029`/`43030`.
- Nine active training workers: `60307`, `60312`, `60319`, `60328`, `60337`, `60345`, `60727`, `68152`, `93382`.
- Workers were consuming approximately 46–48% CPU each.

Running the full build under that load was not operationally safe. Consequently, final Xcode linkage and built-executable architecture remain the only Phase 6D mechanical verification gap.

Exact deferred command:

```bash
xcodebuild \
  -project ExpertAdvisor.xcodeproj \
  -scheme "LSTM Release" \
  -configuration Release \
  -derivedDataPath DerivedData/ExpertAdvisor \
  build
```

No scheduler command or `LSTM_Release` executable was invoked. No processes were signaled or interrupted. No production `LSTM` database connection occurred. All disposable databases, schemas, test binaries, and temporary files were removed. Nothing was staged, committed, pushed, or rewritten.

## Recommendation

Do not stage Phase 6D yet. Project architecture must first define the exact second governance decision—such as an explicitly named ratification or separately scoped approval—with its role, eligibility, and distinction from Phase 6C encoded consistently in terminology, types, fields, documentation, service semantics, and tests. Then rerun focused verification and the deferred Release build when workers are idle.
